METI / Strategic Intelligence / V2.3
EXECUTIVE READ · 29 OCTOBER 2026

What the external evidence establishes

The public record documents continued VMware commitments, scoped workload migrations and product-specific alternative support. It does not establish a sector-wide VMware exit rate or net VMware customer growth.

2,200Gilead VMware-source servers reported migrated
19Biopharma peers examined
10Virtualization alternatives assessed
9OT/manufacturer source entries
SEVEN EVIDENCE-BACKED FINDINGS
01DOCUMENTED MIGRATION

Actual VMware-origin migration at a major biopharma peer is documented at project scale.

Published evidence

At AWS re:Invent 2024, Gilead's named presenters reported 2,200 VMware-source servers migrated, 1,400 VMware servers decommissioned, 340 applications moved and 1.2 PB of shared-file storage moved to FSxN. The broader 87% data-center footprint migrated is not an 87% VMware exit. [N23:06; R1:S05–S06]

Why it matters

This is the most detailed VMware-specific peer case in the 19-company research. Gilead also cited licensing/contract concerns, modernization and consolidation, without attributing the initiative solely to Broadcom. Residual VMware remains unquantified.

Explore detailed evidence
02BIOPHARMA OPERATIONS

VMware remains explicitly referenced in contemporary biopharma engineering requirements.

Published evidence

Lilly's original global VMware ESX platform-engineering role was discoverable during 2026; Amgen's 13 Oct 2026 WINTEL role names VMware and Acropolis; Teva's 30 Apr 2026 IT Operations role names VMware with Commvault/Azure; original Regeneron industrial IT and Takeda manufacturing-site roles also name VMware. [N23:01–05]

Why it matters

These are employer-stated operating or skills requirements, not five verified 2026 production estates or renewal agreements. They strengthen sector context beyond 2011–2022 historical VMware cases.

Explore detailed evidence
03CUSTOMER RESPONSES

The market contains identifiable VCF commitments and defined VMware departures, but not a measured industry strategy distribution.

Published evidence

LSEG announced a five-year Broadcom agreement in May 2026; ING selected VCF 9 in Dec 2025; Audi described a live VCF-backed factory application in Mar 2025. Separately, Nutanix reports ~2,000 VMware VMs moved at MSIG Asia, Proxmox 320 VMs at Estracom, and Vates 550 VMs at Virtual Computing. [R3:E17; R2:S06, S09; R4:S04, S09, S12]

Why it matters

Contract renewal, operational deployment and migrated workload volume are different measures. None establishes how many VMware customers globally are expanding, retaining a smaller estate, or exiting.

Explore detailed evidence
04INDUSTRIAL / OT

An OT manufacturer now describes an alternative and continued VMware support in the same technical source.

Published evidence

Honeywell's Oct 2025 bulletin states that its Nutanix Premium Platform / EVIR600.1 supports Experion PKS R530; separately it says new VMware systems remain available and Honeywell-issued VMware Embedded OEM Foundation support extends through 13 Jun 2030. [N23:07]

Why it matters

There is a documented product/release-specific alternative, not universal AHV certification or proof customers are migrating. Honeywell's continued VMware support makes any general “OT is exiting VMware” interpretation untenable.

Explore detailed evidence
05BIOPHARMA MANUFACTURING

An actual pharmaceutical manufacturer uses the Nutanix ecosystem for MES modernization, but this is not a confirmed VMware exit.

Published evidence

GC Biopharma, outside the 19-peer cohort, describes Nutanix infrastructure adoption since 2020, eight subsequent expansions, and Nutanix Kubernetes Platform (NKP) for MES at Ochang in a July 2026 customer case. Körber separately reported Nutanix-based MES server infrastructure in May 2025. [N23:08–09]

Why it matters

NKP, Nutanix Cloud Infrastructure (NCI) and AHV are distinct product scopes. This is manufacturing-platform evidence, not a documented VMware-to-AHV migration or universal MES compatibility assertion.

Explore detailed evidence
06BROADCOM ECONOMICS

Broadcom's commercial figures do not resolve customer additions or losses.

Published evidence

FY2025 infrastructure-software revenue increased from $21.478bn to $27.029bn (+26%); Q3 FY2026 software revenue increased $6.786bn to $8.752bn (+29% y/y). Broadcom says >90% of its largest 10,000 VMware customers purchased VCF as of Feb 2026. [R2:S01, S03–S05]

Why it matters

Net VMware customer growth: inconclusive. Software-segment revenue includes other products and is affected by contract recognition/subscription conversion; possible price effects are not isolated. Purchased VCF in an existing cohort is not customer additions or deployed VM counts.

Explore detailed evidence
07MARKET & ALTERNATIVES

Alternative viability and OT compatibility are application- and architecture-specific.

Published evidence

The 2026 S&P 451 primary-platform survey reports ~43% VMware and ~28% Hyper-V, not installed-VM share; named migrations to AHV, Proxmox and Vates demonstrate defined scopes. Proacta, a healthcare/bioinformatics software company, reports approximately 100 VMs moved from VMware to Proxmox across two data-center sites in an Aug 2026 supplier case. [R6:S01–S02; R4:S04, S09, S12; N23:10]

Why it matters

Platform selection preference does not establish relative customer counts or full VCF equivalence. A healthcare-adjacent production migration does not certify biopharma GxP/OT applications.

Explore detailed evidence
Decision conditions

Three paths to evaluate

Retain and optimize VMware

Existing platform/support benefits, supported OT dependencies, migration friction or contract economics favor continuity

Verify real cost, current utilization, stack dependencies, renewal terms and technical lifecycle risk.

Hybrid, targeted substitution

Some workload groups can migrate while critical or uneconomic classes remain supported

Demonstrate migration/rollback, operational parity, supplier support, requalification needs and net economics by segment.

Substantial VMware replacement

Larger estate redesign may be economically/architecturally justified after a validated proof program

Requires comprehensive application support, platform-service parity, resilience, skills, transition financing and acceptable interruption risk.

Company-authored engineering responsibility; posting discoverable in 2026, precise posting date not established. Azure is separately named.

METI | Broadcom & VMware — Strategic Decision Intelligence

Documented Infrastructure Choices, Supplier Alternatives and Manufacturing Compatibility

Version: 2.3 — evidence-enriched leadership review Prepared: 29 October 2026 Evidence basis: Dated original financial filings, customer/supplier cases, employer-authored engineering records, published research and original OT manufacturer documentation. New material sources are separately identified in Annex G. Audience: Cloud & Infrastructure Technology Leadership — global biopharma Scope: Public external information, predominantly 2019–2026, with older explicitly dated material. No assumptions regarding internal systems, contracts, suppliers or technology decisions. Evidence standard: Findings draw on original publications. Supplier-reported customer outcomes are attributed, not independently audited. Secondary-only references remain in the appendices and are not presented as established deployments. Prepared by: METI | External technology intelligence assessment.

Executive Summary

As of 29 October 2026. Original company statements, supplier/customer documentation and industrial technical guidance are distinguished from employer hiring requirements and survey responses. The findings below are attributable to the named publication and date, not estimates of worldwide VMware customer behavior.

01DOCUMENTED MIGRATION

Actual VMware-origin migration at a major biopharma peer is documented at project scale.

Published evidence

At AWS re:Invent 2024, Gilead's named presenters reported 2,200 VMware-source servers migrated, 1,400 VMware servers decommissioned, 340 applications moved and 1.2 PB of shared-file storage moved to FSxN. The broader 87% data-center footprint migrated is not an 87% VMware exit. [N23:06; R1:S05–S06]

Why it matters

07MARKET & ALTERNATIVES

Alternative viability and OT compatibility are application- and architecture-specific.

Published evidence

The 2026 S&P 451 primary-platform survey reports ~43% VMware and ~28% Hyper-V, not installed-VM share; named migrations to AHV, Proxmox and Vates demonstrate defined scopes. Proacta, a healthcare/bioinformatics software company, reports approximately 100 VMs moved from VMware to Proxmox across two data-center sites in an Aug 2026 supplier case. [R6:S01–S02; R4:S04, S09, S12; N23:10]

Why it matters

Platform selection preference does not establish relative customer counts or full VCF equivalence. A healthcare-adjacent production migration does not certify biopharma GxP/OT applications.

Explore detailed evidence

Why it matters

The documented pattern is not a sector-wide VMware exit or unquestioned retention. Gilead provides quantified VMware-source migration evidence; other biopharma companies publish VMware-related engineering requirements without publishing the underlying active estate. Honeywell's named Experion/Nutanix support illustrates that specific OT options can broaden while VMware remains supported. These distinct evidence categories must not be added into a retention or churn statistic. [N23:01–09; R1:S05–S06; R6:S03]

For Cloud Leadership, the defensible next decision is a workload-by-workload comparison of VMware optimization, selective replacement and larger-scale substitution against manufacturer support, platform-service coverage, recoverability and lifecycle economics. Neither a universal VCF replacement nor the preferred option for an undisclosed estate is established by these sources. [R4:S04–S18; R5:OT01–OT10; N23:07]

1. Observed Customer Behavior: Commitments, Workload Movements and Residual Dependence

1.1 Named contracts, selections and deployed uses

Published customer and supplier records: The following cases distinguish agreements, product selections and reported deployment stages rather than treating them as identical enterprise-wide commitments.

Customer Publicly reported action Interpretation boundary
Audi March 2025: selected VCF-supported virtual-PLC/factory automation deployment live at Böllinger Höfe. [R2:S06] Specific manufacturing deployment, not global factory share.
Walmart August 2025: strategic supplier collaboration and prospective VCF cloud/edge plan. [R2:S07] Planned rollout is not completed rollout.
Lloyds Banking Group September 2025: multi-year Broadcom software expansion including VCF. [R2:S08] Contract continuity, not VM growth.
ING December 2025: VCF 9 selected for private-cloud modernization. [R2:S09] Selection, not verified full implementation.
LSEG May 2026: five-year VCF agreement; rollout described as planned. [R2:S10] Explicit continued relationship, scope and deployment distinct.
Standard Chartered July 2026: supplier/customer reports 70% of global infrastructure footprint on its new architecture. [R2:S11] Customer-reported figure, not independently audited.
NTT DOCOMO October 2026: announced ten-year VMware software relationship. [R2:S16] Official publication indexed; full reopening unsuccessful in underlying run.
FedEx October 2026: supplier says it continues to use VCF and other Broadcom products. [R2:S17] Official publication indexed; full reopening unsuccessful in underlying run.

Customer commitment: what the cases establish

The cases establish contractual renewal, platform selection, intended rollout and selected operational implementation, not a common enterprise-wide VCF adoption result. Customer-specific deployed capacity and subsequent contract outcomes remain separate evidence categories. The selected announcements cannot be used to infer worldwide customer retention or increase. [R2:S06–S11, S16–S17]

Gilead Sciences — migration execution, scale and stated drivers (AWS re:Invent 2024)

The AWS-hosted MAM214 presentation names Gilead's Cloud Delivery & Support and IT Security Architecture leaders as presenters. The slide on VMware migration outcomes reports the following publisher/customer-attributed, not independently audited, figures:

Disclosed migration measure Reported value Scope and interpretation
Servers migrated from VMware to AWS 2,200 VMware-source workloads; no residual enterprise VMware total
VMware servers decommissioned 1,400 Server retirement; not an additional 1,400 migrations to add to 2,200
Applications migrated to AWS 340 Applications described on the VMware migration outcome slide; not a count of GxP-qualified systems
Shared-file storage migrated to AWS FSxN 1.2 PB File-storage workload; cannot be interpreted as hypervisor capacity
Wider data-center footprint migrated 87% Timeline chart refers to overall data-center migration including VMware, not VMware exit percentage

Gilead-attributed program drivers: the original presentation identifies licensing-cost increases, risk/disruption and complex contracts alongside technical debt, security/reliability, cloud modernization, data-center consolidation and other operating concerns. It explicitly contemplates retaining some workloads on premises. The source does not establish that Broadcom's acquisition alone caused the migration. N23:06

Assurance: Original AWS-hosted slide-deck text and the public AWS session description were retrieved. The source PDF's automated screenshot service failed, so the detailed slide figures are cited as original presentation text, not independently audited or visually reverified. Numeric wording reconciliation: The 2024 presentation labels decommissioned VMware servers as 1,400; AWS's published event description uses 1,400+. They are publisher presentations of the same program at different precision, not independently reconciled exact counts. The broader statement of over 2,000 migrated remains in the original R1 register. [R1:S05–S06; N23:06]

Customer migrations: what the cases establish

Suppliers and named customers report completed or underway source-to-destination migrations for defined workloads. In contrast, a target platform's customer case without identified VMware origin does not establish displacement. Neither migration VM counts nor seller-reported savings should be summed into a global rate or accepted as independently audited. [R1:S05–S06; R4:S04–S13, S17]

1.3 Evidence of reducing VMware dependence while retaining it

The original publisher's separate 2026 S&P 451 private-cloud study reports approximately one quarter of current VMware users shifting some workloads to alternative private-cloud platforms while keeping a reduced VMware estate; around one third evaluating or transitioning; and 6% undecided. These were survey responses about approaches, not independently observed migration completion, and should not be merged with the separate primary-platform survey. R6:S03

Original ETR Research also reported respondent-level cost and migration-strategy concerns in 2026. That is a survey of respondents, not a named verified completed migration. R3:E11

VMware reduction: survey evidence and limitations

Partial diversification is supported as a stated strategy, while the named migration cases demonstrate scoped implementation. The research does not verify a representative share of worldwide VMware customers who have both migrated workloads and continued operating a residual VMware estate. This is the relevant distinction behind mixed external accounts. [R6:S03; R1:S05–S06; R4:S04, S09–S13]

Research period: 2019–2026 primary lookback, retaining separately dated earlier evidence (including Sanofi's 2012 vSphere deployment and AstraZeneca's 2017 VMware ESX environment). The 19 companies comprise a scoped comparison, not a representative industry sample or exhaustive inventory of private contracts.

What the 19-company evidence establishes

The new evidence strengthens the current-period operational picture, not a current-customer census. Lilly's company-hosted posting explicitly describes global on-premises ESX operations; Amgen, Regeneron, Takeda and Teva separately name VMware in 2026-accessible technical roles. These are employer requirements, with unequal strength: some specify operating responsibilities, while others merely ask for experience. None provides VMware host counts, 2026 VCF renewals or migration strategy. [N23:01–05]

Historical deployment and post-acquisition movement are distinct. Roche Diagnostics (VMware original case), AstraZeneca (Morpheus original case) and Sanofi (Dell/EMC original publication) provide dated VMware use; Gilead is the one quantified VMware-origin migration among these 19 peers meeting the case threshold. Cloud partnerships elsewhere do not prove VMware replacement. The other 18 are unclassified for a documented post-acquisition VMware strategic outcome, not assumed VMware retainers or non-users. [R1:S05–S06, S30; BP01–BP02; N23:06]

2.2 What the biopharma cases establish

Original publication Established fact Interpretation limit
AWS/Gilead re:Invent MAM214 [R1:S05–S06] AWS reports >2,000 on-premises VMware-source servers migrated and >1,400 systems retired. Separate counts; no complete VMware exit or causal finding about Broadcom.
VMware/Roche Diagnostics 2022 [R1:S30] ESXi/vCenter/Horizon and VMware Cloud on AWS in simulation/training. A historical specialized workload, not 2026 renewal or general GxP certification.

GC Biopharma — named manufacturing-platform comparator outside the 19 peers

Original-source observations: A July 2026 Nutanix named customer case states that GC Biopharma adopted Nutanix infrastructure in 2020, expanded it eight times, and adopted Nutanix Kubernetes Platform (NKP) for a core MES modernization at Ochang. Nutanix also identifies NCI and Prism among associated products; this does not identify the MES hypervisor as AHV or prove VMware origin. A separate 19 May 2025 Körber original supplier announcement describes PAS-X MES implementation and a partner delivering Nutanix-based MES servers; the two sources may describe related projects but must not be collapsed into a single audited implementation milestone. [N23:08–09]

Finding: Named biopharma MES modernization on Nutanix technology is documented; VMware displacement, GxP certification of a particular hypervisor, and applicability to the original 19-company cohort are not established. This is an adjacent sector comparator, not a 20th peer or counted VMware migration. [N23:08–09]

Biopharma decisions: limits of inference

Biopharma cloud-provider choices, current engineering requirements, manufacturing-platform adoption and VMware-source migrations are separate observations. Without an original VMware source, renewal or decommissioning record, the public cloud initiatives of a peer cannot be classified as Broadcom retention or exit. The disclosed cases demonstrate different workload types; they are not a comparable sample of post-acquisition VMware decisions. [R1:S05–S11; R1:S30]

Primary-platform preference, installed presence and intended future approaches have different denominators and must remain separate. [R6:S01–S03]

Alternative platforms: documented scope and limitations

Additional case distinction: GC Biopharma's Nutanix NKP MES modernization and NCI infrastructure evidence are not synonymous with an AHV virtualization migration. Proacta's Proxmox VE/Backup Server migration is a named healthcare/bioinformatics IT estate example, not product-specific OT support. The additional records strengthen the set of relevant implementation analogues without providing uniform full-VCF parity or comparative global market share. [N23:08–10]

Alternative hypervisors and infrastructure stacks have documented capabilities and some named VMware-source migrations for defined workloads. These original supplier records do not establish general financial superiority, comparable global customer share, or universal full-VCF replacement. Product scope, migration destination and support ecosystem differ by option. [R4:S04–S18; R5:S21–S33]

Hypervisor replacement versus full VCF-stack replacement

VMware dependency / service Required comparison for an alternative Risk if unproven
ESXi/vSphere compute and guests Supported OS and virtual hardware, CPU/GPU, migration tools, licensing and workload performance VM runs but application behavior or support differs
vCenter/operations Provisioning, API, policy, RBAC, automation, inventory and lifecycle Fragmented operating model and change control
vSAN/storage Storage performance, snapshots, encryption, failure domains, recovery and hardware compatibility Resilience/performance or storage architecture gap
NSX/network/security Segmentation, network policies, virtual switching, identity and security tooling Lost controls or rework of network/security design
Availability, recovery and observability HA/DR, backup restore, RPO/RTO, testing, monitoring and incident support Business interruption or unsupported recovery claims
OT/GxP application dependencies Exact manufacturer product/version support and qualification Unsupported or unqualified production workloads
Commercial and operating model License/support, new hardware, migration services, dual run, training, supplier durability Apparent license saving offset by transition/operating cost

Hypervisor operation is a narrower capability than the VMware management, networking/security, storage, recovery and automation services potentially present in a VCF estate. Comparing an alternative against the actually used services is an analyst assessment framework; the existing cases do not independently establish total-stack parity. [R4:S06, S08, S14, S23]

Siemens WinCC Runtime Unified V21 VMware and Hyper-V in product-specific installation guidance. [R5:OT02] Specific tested combinations and networking constraints govern.
Rockwell FactoryTalk Services Platform 6.60.00 General vendor guidance names VMware, Hyper-V, Nutanix. [R5:OT03] Rockwell expressly defers to product-specific documentation and support terms; no blanket AHV approval.
Schneider EcoStruxure Control Expert V15.3 VMware ESXi, Workstation 15.5.2, Oracle VirtualBox 6.1. [R5:OT04] Historical tested environments; VirtualBox is not Oracle Linux KVM/OLVM.
Schneider Data Center Expert virtual appliance ESXi 6.7+, Hyper-V on Windows Server 2019/2022 for specified DCE versions. [R5:OT05] Infrastructure-management appliance, not evidence of SCADA/control certification.

Emerson DeltaV v15 FP3 virtual SZ VMware and Hyper-V for specified simulation/training/virtual FAT functions. [R5:OT10] Not approval for all production DCS control nodes.
Honeywell Experion PKS R530 — Premium Platform for Nutanix, EVIR600.1 Honeywell's October 2025 System HINTS identifies Nutanix support for EVIR600.1 with Experion PKS R530 and says the detailed HPS Virtualization Specification governs. [N23:07] Manufacturer-published product/release-specific platform support; not universal AHV support for all Honeywell applications, nor proof any named biopharma customer has deployed it.
Honeywell Experion PKS — VMware support / Honeywell Embedded OEM The same original bulletin states Honeywell continues to qualify VMware for its applications and offers new VMware-based systems. It specifies Honeywell-issued VMware Embedded OEM Foundation support coverage through 13 June 2030, subject to its support arrangement. [N23:07] Continued manufacturer support alongside a specified Nutanix alternative; not evidence that all Honeywell customers retain VMware or that Broadcom contracts across biopharma were renewed.

Limit of the evidence: The cited AVEVA original publication is a guide announcement; complete guide clauses were not inspected. Rockwell's general FactoryTalk documentation names VMware, Hyper-V and Nutanix but defers to application-specific restrictions. Emerson's cited DeltaV virtual SZ release concerns simulation/training/virtual FAT, not broad production DCS eligibility. [R5:OT03, OT09–OT10]

OT application support: documented scope

Honeywell is a materially different evidence class from a general migration case. Its own bulletin identifies a specific Experion PKS/Nutanix release and continuing VMware support. The manufacturer does not thereby establish certification of all Honeywell nodes, all MES/DCS applications, or all Nutanix product configurations. The original PDF text was retrieved, but the automated screenshot service failed; release wording and support coverage are reproduced with that assurance qualification. [N23:07]

The original documents establish particular manufacturer-published hypervisor references, not enterprise-wide OT compatibility. A Hyper-V-supported application is not automatically Azure Local-approved; generic KVM product capability is not manufacturer approval of every KVM platform; historical or simulation support is not current plant control certification. [R5:OT01–OT05, OT09–OT10]

The existing documentation does not cover every MES, SCADA/HMI, historian, DCS, laboratory and supporting industrial product. An actual change would require version-specific publisher support, guest/host/I/O verification, operational recovery design, and qualification where relevant.

6. Why it matters

Strategic path Potential rationale Conditions that would change the decision
Retain and optimize VMware Existing platform/support benefits, supported OT dependencies, migration friction or contract economics favor continuity Verify real cost, current utilization, stack dependencies, renewal terms and technical lifecycle risk.
Hybrid, targeted substitution Some workload groups can migrate while critical or uneconomic classes remain supported Demonstrate migration/rollback, operational parity, supplier support, requalification needs and net economics by segment.
Substantial VMware replacement Larger estate redesign may be economically/architecturally justified after a validated proof program Requires comprehensive application support, platform-service parity, resilience, skills, transition financing and acceptable interruption risk.

Conditions for an enterprise decision

The public evidence supports evaluating three alternative paths, not selecting a supplier or assuming either wholesale retention or replacement. Exact VMware stack dependencies, workload criticality, supplier support, migration/restoration tests and risk-adjusted economics are required before those paths can be distinguished for a particular enterprise. [R4:S04–S18; R5:OT01–OT10]

Gate Evidence required Decision enabled
1. Current estate and contract baseline VMware/VCF products, powered-on VMs, hosts/cores, versions, dependencies and renewal terms Define the actual replacement problem, including software purchased versus deployed
2. Workload segmentation Corporate, SAP/data, scientific/HPC, dev/test, edge, manufacturing/OT and GxP classes; owner and criticality Identify candidate, protected and unknown workloads
3. Competitive technical validation Select platform subset; service parity for management, network, storage, backup, DR and IAM Determine which alternatives are credible for each class
4. OEM/OT support and qualification Exact application and release compatibility, manufacturer statements, Quality/change-impact checks Exclude or govern unsupported/uncertified production migrations
5. Migration and recovery proof Pilot conversions, dependent services, downtime, data integrity, backup/restore and rollback Establish feasibility and operational acceptance
6. Comparable full economics 3–5-year software, hardware, services, migration, dual-run, skills, DR, validation and exit costs Compare optimize, selective diversification and substantial replacement under common assumptions

Final assessment

The refreshed biopharma record now contains three distinct signals: dated VMware deployments, employer-described VMware engineering requirements accessible in 2026, and a quantified Gilead migration. This does not establish a cohort-wide 2026 customer response. Outside biopharma, documented contractual continuity, workload migration and survey intentions likewise measure different decisions. Original source publications show that all three occur, but do not establish how common each is among worldwide VMware customers or biopharma peers. Broadcom's net VMware customer growth is inconclusive; the existence of named migration cases does not show a sector-wide exit. [R2:S01, S03, S06–S11; R6:S03; R1:S05–S06; R4:S04, S09–S13]

The Honeywell and GC Biopharma evidence makes application-specific alternative assessment more concrete, but does not show full VCF or sector-wide OT substitution. The practical question remains which workloads and surrounding operating services can be substituted with manufacturer support, recovery assurance and full lifecycle economics. The reviewed originals support a competitive assessment; they do not resolve an undisclosed enterprise's optimal outcome. [R4:S04–S18; R5:OT01–OT10]

Original Nutanix customer case MSIG Asia VMware to AHV, approximately 2,000 VMs. R4:S04 Supplier-hosted customer statement, not independent industry adoption census.
Original Proxmox/Vates/VergeIO cases Defined VMware-source migration programs and stated outcomes. R4:S09–S13 Original supplier/customer stories, not independently audited savings or enterprise-wide parity.
Original VMware, Morpheus, Dell named cases Roche Diagnostics 2022, AstraZeneca historical ESX, Sanofi 2012 vSphere. R1:S30; BP01–BP02 Historical original supplier publications, not active 2026 contracts.
Original OT application manufacturer documents Siemens/Rockwell/Schneider/AVEVA/Emerson product-specific virtualization guidance. R5:OT01–OT05, OT09–OT10 Application/release scope. AVEVA original is announcement only, not full guide.
Original ETR Research publication 2026 observed survey respondents' VMware cost and platform plans. R3:E11 Respondent study, not audited customer migration.

Primary-source convention: A supplier's own named customer case is an original supplier publication, but is not independent verification of customer counts, savings or operational performance. Original researcher survey figures describe the survey, not the market census. A company-authored statement about another cloud workload does not demonstrate VMware removal. Historical source-access statuses remain exactly as recorded in the V2.1 annexes.

Excluded from primary-confirmed deployment statements: BP03 (secondary published J&J account), BP04 (third-party-hosted Pfizer transcript), BP05 (commercial AstraZeneca CA Clarity directory). These remain accessible as original research leads in Annex E, not deleted or silently converted into facts.

Original METI evidence appendices A–F (unchanged)

Research Annexes — Supporting Detail and Audit Trail

The annexes are intended for analysts, architects and reviewers. They contain the original research's technical details, source ledgers and claim-level qualifications. Historical source-access notes are retained for traceability; they are not current verification claims.

Annex A — Nineteen-company factual dossiers (original Report 1)

The following original dossiers preserve the nuanced company-by-company source and scope distinctions behind Section 5.

5. Company factual dossiers

Each dossier records the latest fact that survived the accessible-source check, not a guessed enterprise state.

5.1 Eli Lilly and Company

VMware footprint/response: Not established. The earlier Workday vacancy R-107936 included references to ESX/VMware in search indexing, but direct original-page retrieval failed [X01]. It is excluded as an accessible confirmed-footprint source. Other infrastructure: Uptimal describes a Lilly AWS data automation implementation using AWS Lambda and existing AWS infrastructure; this is partner-authored and limited to business intelligence pipelines [S23]. What cannot be concluded: Hypervisor footprint, Azure/VMware coexistence, 2026 VCF renewal, use of AWS as primary cloud, or VMware migration.

5.2 Johnson & Johnson

VMware footprint/response: Not established in inspected public material. Other infrastructure: J&J's company release, dated before the acquisition, identifies Microsoft's cloud/Azure as preferred for MedTech digital surgery, not the pharma division or corporate enterprise [S14]. Financial source: J&J's FY2025 results were opened [S04]; the inspected materials did not provide a VMware customer-estate record. Limitation: The business-unit and historical timing preclude any claim about a 2026 global J&J VMware strategy.

5.3 AbbVie

VMware evidence: An AbbVie-authored expired job listing states responsibility for VMware/Windows data-center operations, platform security, problem resolution and VMware lifecycle management [S07]. It mentions servers in cloud, operational technology and data-center environments, but does not furnish deployment counts. Post-Broadcom response: No renewal, VCF rollout, reduction or migration verified. Evidence nature: C1, *expired recruitment requirement*, not audited production evidence. Limitations: The listing is accessible but expired and has no sufficiently established posting/event date to treat it as proof of current installations.

5.4 Roche Holding

Historical VMware footprint: VMware's 3 August 2022 Roche Diagnostics customer case describes a training/simulation estate using ESXi, vCenter, Horizon, Workspace ONE Access and VMware Cloud on AWS [S30]. It reports >15,000 VM consumptions per month, a utilization metric rather than a current or concurrent VM count. This is historical vendor-hosted customer evidence, not a 2026 Broadcom contract or renewal. Post-acquisition VMware response: No qualifying response established. Infrastructure: Roche's 16 March 2026 company release says it added 2,176 on-premises NVIDIA Blackwell GPUs across the US and Europe; combined on-premises and cloud capacity exceeds 3,500 Blackwell GPUs [S09]. Scope: AI compute across Roche operations. Interpretation: Demonstrates willingness to expand privately operated high-performance infrastructure for particular workloads. Not evidence of VCF, AHV or general VM hosting. Limits: No VMware licensing or hypervisor architecture is disclosed.

5.5 Novartis

VMware: No verifiable post-acquisition decision or estate size. Infrastructure: AWS's named Novartis case documents patient-service workloads and cloud platform services, with a 2024 program roadmap [S15]. A supplier description of AWS+Azure analytics exists, but the direct source failed opening in this run and is not elevated [X04]. Limits: AWS use cannot be generalized to the entire enterprise or interpreted as VMware migration.

5.6 AstraZeneca

VMware: Not established. Infrastructure: AWS's named customer hub documents genomics research and use of AWS for critical activities, while a separate AWS case describes EC2/EKS compute supporting augmented drug design [S16, S25]. Limits: Case-study publication date and deployment date are not uniformly provided. Neither explicitly states VMware source infrastructure.

5.7 Amgen

VMware: No customer-verified migration or 2026 footprint established. A previously referenced Cognizant VMware-to-AWS business-case illustration is not evidence of an executed migration [X05]. Infrastructure: AWS case describes Amgen's genomics workflows, including an initial on-premises-to-AWS transition dating to 2019 [S17]. AWS's pharma-manufacturing directory refers to Amgen SAP-on-AWS migration [S24] but is insufficient for detailed VMware attribution. Financial record: 2025 Form 10-K reports capitalized cloud-computing-arrangement implementation costs of $272m at 2025 year-end vs $227m at 2024 year-end; these are not VMware costs [S03]. Limits: No verified VCF contract, hypervisor decision, or executed 2,269-VM migration.

5.8 Gilead Sciences

VMware: AWS's 2024 re:Invent MAM214 published session description identifies migration of more than 2,000 servers from on-premises VMware to native AWS services and retirement of more than 1,400 systems [S05]. AWS's 18 Feb 2025 session recap repeats these figures [S06]. Response/stage: Migrate/replace within disclosed workload scope; AWS reports executed migration. Evidence caveat: Both accessible documents are AWS-authored session descriptions. They corroborate the public AWS reporting but are not independent customer-authored proof. The inaccessible Gilead PDF was not treated as a validated direct link, and the more exact 2,200/340 figures were not used. Limits: No full VMware exit, residual host count, specific VCF license reduction, GxP workload transition or causal exclusivity of Broadcom established.

5.9 Novo Nordisk

VMware: No disclosed post-acquisition strategy. Infrastructure: In its 10 Aug 2026 corporate release, Novo Nordisk explicitly designates AWS its preferred cloud provider and strategic AI partner, and describes a London co-innovation hub [S10]. AWS also describes manufacturing data/IoT examples [S12]. Stage: Commercial/strategic partnership and documented application initiatives. Limits: Preferred does not mean exclusive; no VMware migration evidence.

5.10 Pfizer

VMware: Not established. Infrastructure: AWS's PACT case records 14 projects/prototypes spanning research and manufacturing analytics, dating from a collaboration begun in 2021. Its 16,000-hour time-savings and 55% infrastructure-cost figures are presented partly as estimates, not audited realized company-wide effects [S18]. Limits: Neither PACT nor Azure/AWS usage establishes VMware retention/exit.

5.11 Vertex Pharmaceuticals

Infrastructure: AWS describes cryo-EM processing/storage shifted from on-premises HPC beginning around 2022, with AWS EC2/ParallelCluster/FSx, plus a separately described AWS Step Functions serverless image-processing redesign in April 2023 [S19, S20]. Stage: Named technical implementation, pre-acquisition. Limits: Previous on-premises HPC systems were not identified as VMware; no Broadcom-era inference permitted.

5.12 Bristol Myers Squibb

VMware: Not established. Infrastructure: AWS/Accenture's 2021 case documents BMS's SAP ECC-to-S/4HANA conversion on AWS across 44 markets, 12 manufacturing sites and 13,000 users [S21]; a BMS cloud engineering guest-authored blog in 2022 describes AWS account provisioning automation [S22]. Stage: Historical implemented enterprise and cloud platform capabilities. Limits: Pre-2023 program cannot be dated as a reaction to Broadcom or as VMware displacement.

5.13 GSK

VMware: No verified renewal/exit. Other virtualization/hybrid: Microsoft explicitly identifies GSK as an Azure Local customer enabling edge processing and AI inference across vaccine and medicine manufacturing and R&D laboratories; the article also references participation in Microsoft Ignite [S11]. Stage: Microsoft-reported customer use (V1); conference session itself was not independently reviewed. Limits: No public proof that Azure Local displaced a specific VMware site, VM count or VCF agreement.

5.14 Sanofi

VMware: No documented decision. Infrastructure: AWS's Sanofi Digital Accelerator customer case describes AWS-hosted serverless, ML and data capabilities used across clinical, R&D, commercial and manufacturing-related digital solutions [S26]. The program began in 2022. Limits: Application-platform evidence, not enterprise virtualization or hypervisor replacement. Customer quotations appear within a vendor-hosted case.

5.15 Regeneron Pharmaceuticals

VMware: No directly verified post-acquisition strategy or footprint; older indexed job references are not counted. Other verified-scope cloud evidence: A Google Cloud Moderna customer story documents Looker and Amazon Redshift use in a data architecture [S32]; this does not establish a mixed hypervisor estate. Annex B — Additional biopharma case archetypes (original Report 3)

These case narratives distinguish a historical VMware relationship, a named migration, hybrid-edge implementations and cloud/data workload examples.

4. Detailed evidence-led company archetypes

4.1 Roche Diagnostics: a real historical VMware estate—and the limits of the record

Customer and platform: VMware's original 3 August 2022 case names Roche Diagnostics, its platform owner, ESXi/vCenter/Horizon/Workspace ONE Access, an on-premises training simulation system, and VMware Cloud on AWS expansion in the United States and Europe [E01]. The narrative reports more than 6,000 users on the on-premises system, over 15,000 virtual-machine consumptions per month, and three VMware Cloud on AWS hosts at each of two documented locations at that time. These are *2022 case-study figures*, not a 2026 live-estate measurement.

Why significant: Specialized training environments running multiple versions of diagnostics software represent a concrete example of virtualization serving biopharma-adjacent product/customer support with persistence, legacy-OS and regional considerations—not just general ERP hosting.

2026 adjacent change: Roche itself announced additional on-premises NVIDIA GPU capacity in March 2026 [E05]. The announcement does not identify a hypervisor or say those systems replaced VMware. A Roche on-premises GPU deployment and a historical VMware VDI estate are not proof of a unified modern Roche infrastructure decision.

Executive applicability: Historical example of a specialized/long-lived use case that may demand different migration logic from standard application servers. Do not cite the 15,000 monthly instances as 15,000 simultaneously deployed VMs or Roche-wide production VM footprint.

4.2 Gilead: observed source-to-destination transformation

AWS's MAM214 session guide and playlist were opened and inspected [E03]. They describe migration from on-premises VMware to AWS native services, with over 2,000 servers migrated and over 1,400 systems retired. This is concrete displacement inside the identified migration program.

Limitations: The original slide PDF previously supplied returned Access Denied and was not validated as a live accessible primary document here. Therefore the more exact 2,200-server and 340-application figures are not adopted in this edition. A migrated workload may be retained in a new form, while a retired system may be part of the program: do not add the two numbers. The record does not prove that Gilead's remaining VMware footprint is zero.

4.3 GSK: an alternative on-premises/hybrid execution pattern

Microsoft explicitly names GSK as using Azure Local for real-time data processing and AI inference spanning vaccine/medicine manufacturing and R&D laboratories [E04]. This is a meaningful implementation signal for pharmaceutical edge/site compute. But the source does not establish the virtual-machine count, physical site count, GxP validation scope, VMware contract, or whether Azure Local displaced VMware.

4.4 Novo Nordisk: declared preferred provider coexists with other real cloud workloads

Novo Nordisk's 10 August 2026 company release calls AWS the preferred cloud provider and strategic AI partner [E06]. A Microsoft May 2026 customer record details an Azure-hosted clinical-research analytics agent and its FounData setting [E07]. An October 2024 Microsoft case details Azure AI/data infrastructure [E22]. Together, these records affirm *multiple platforms used for distinct business purposes*. They do not describe two competing hypervisors, nor make AWS the sole hosting provider.

4.5 Moderna: documented multicloud data, not validated VMware VM platform

Google Cloud's named Moderna case describes Looker operating with data centered in Amazon Redshift, alongside quotations from a Moderna technology executive [E08]. VMware's historic life-sciences promotional brief quotes Moderna's CEO about VMware's usefulness [E02], but does not identify the VMware product, server clusters, quantities or date of a VM deployment. The two sources are not evidence of a post-2023 VMware retention or exit decision.

5. VMware's published customer base: what can be reconstructed

5.1 Is there a complete, public Broadcom list of every VMware customer?

No comprehensive open, dated, customer-by-customer Broadcom installed-base list was identified. Public Broadcom evidence comprises selectively named references, investor-reported aggregate sales/conversion statistics and hosted customer stories.

  • Broadcom's official February 2026 VCF publication states more than 90% of its 10,000 largest VMware customers purchased VCF; it names Audi, ING, Lloyds and Walmart as examples [E09]. This is commercial-purchasing penetration within a defined selected cohort, not VCF deployment, all-customer retention, or a list of customer identities.
  • Broadcom's FY2025 10-K reports broader infrastructure-software segment results and describes VCF and revenue recognition drivers; these results encompass other infrastructure-software offerings and do not name 19-company VCF purchases [E10].
  • VMware's older customer pages, including Roche Diagnostics, can establish past named customer relationships. They do not prove survival, renewal or a 2026 installed base.

5.2 Commercial customer directories

Apps Run The World — VMware vSphere customer directory and its VCF customer directory were opened [E14–E15]. They explicitly describe combining public and proprietary research, with partial public previews and paid filters. Their preview did not furnish attributable records for the 19 named firms in this check. Thus they are research leads, not independent proof of a dated 2026 installation at one of the 19.

Public record triangulation standard: A directory hit would require an underlying vendor case, customer statement, procurement entry or technical record proving scope and date before being promoted to a verified footprint claim.

Evaluating only by logo-level “stay/move” masks these distinctions.

7. Independent and competitor market evidence

Annex C — Competitor technical dossiers and OT compatibility catalogs (original Report 5)

Detailed product and integration notes are retained to support vendor assessment; they are not a ranking or proof of application certification.

4. Detailed competitive dossiers

4.1 Nutanix Cloud Infrastructure / AHV

Documented: Nutanix's FY2026 10-K reports over 32,000 end customers at July 31, 2026, subject to its definition that an organization can count more than once for subsidiaries/divisions. Its customer story says MSIG Asia, motivated partly by VMware licensing costs, transitioned approximately 2,000 VMs to Nutanix AHV by April 2025. [S03, S06]

Assessment: Among the more credible enterprise replacements for a substantial vSphere/vSAN estate, especially where an HCI architecture and unified operations model are desired. The customer example is evidence of production migration, but not a global success rate or savings guarantee. Verify applications, storage performance, GPU needs, network security, DR tooling, end-to-end TCO and contract terms.

Key trade-off: Replacing VMware vendor concentration with a new tightly integrated platform can reduce some operational layers while increasing dependency on Nutanix's software and compatible hardware ecosystem.

4.2 Microsoft Hyper-V and Azure Local

Documented: Microsoft documents live migration and clustering for Hyper-V and an Azure Migrate process for VMware VMs to Azure Local. Its release notes state VMware-to-Azure-Local migration became generally available in October 2025. Azure Local migration requires coordinated source/target appliances; Microsoft documents third-party migration options as well. [S07, S08]

Assessment: Highly relevant for Windows-oriented corporate application estates, regional datacenters and hybrid/cloud-governed environments. Needs careful distinction between standalone Hyper-V licensing and Azure Local subscription/service requirements.

Key trade-off: Familiarity for Microsoft workloads and tools does not establish free migration, one-for-one operational equivalence, or support for all regulated applications and devices.

4.3 Red Hat OpenShift Virtualization

Documented: Red Hat states OpenShift Virtualization runs VM workloads alongside containers and supplies a migration toolkit that can migrate from VMware vSphere, RHV and OpenStack. [S09]

Assessment: Particularly viable when the target operating model already centers on OpenShift/Kubernetes and the organization wants to converge VM and container operations. A first phase could include Linux business applications and selected non-production workloads.

Key trade-off: Organizations without OpenShift expertise may trade VMware administration for a substantially new platform operations model. Backup, storage, networking and specialized application compatibility cannot be assumed.

4.4 HPE Morpheus VM Essentials / HVM

Documented: HPE's technical QuickSpecs describe HVM KVM, HA, live migration, external storage and a unified manager for HVM and VMware; product documentation describes bulk migration from vCenter to HVM clusters. [S10]

Assessment: A useful migration bridge where organizations need a gradual replacement or a mixed-hypervisor operational model rather than a one-time cutover. It may be especially relevant where HPE infrastructure, support and procurement relationships already exist.

Key trade-off: The offering should be tested as the particular VM Essentials SKU, not confused with higher-tier Morpheus capabilities or with older Morpheus cloud-management customer references. Validate support for NSX-type controls, DR and desired vSAN replacement.

4.5 Proxmox Virtual Environment

Documented: Official product documentation details KVM, LXC, clustered HA, live migration, software-defined networking, Ceph storage, REST API and Proxmox Backup Server. Its September 2026 announcement states more than 2.3 million active Proxmox VE servers and 24/7 vendor-direct enterprise support effective October 19, 2026; the announcement was discovered on the official site but could not be reopened directly in the research session. [S11, S12]

Assessment: A technically serious alternative, particularly for Linux-capable teams, research compute, development environments and defined enterprise clusters. It cannot be dismissed simply because it is open source. Nevertheless, deployment count is not enterprise penetration, and regulated vendors may support only nominated virtualization platforms.

Key trade-off: Lower software licensing exposure may be offset by greater integration, staffing, support and specialized certification burden.

4.6 Oracle Linux Virtualization Manager

Documented: Oracle documents an Oracle Linux KVM/oVirt-based management stack with host/VM management, storage/network domains, cluster HA and live migration. Oracle's lifecycle pages identify version 4.5 on Oracle Linux 9 as released August 2026. [S13]

Assessment: Relevant to Oracle and Linux application estates when vendor support, processor licensing and Linux platform alignment are material. It should not be assumed that KVM automatically satisfies every Oracle application certification or licensing requirement; these require exact contractual and ISV validation.

Key trade-off: More modular than VMware VCF, but requires integration of additional cloud-operations, DR and networking functions.

4.7 Canonical MicroCloud / OpenStack

Documented: Canonical's Tennessee Tech case reports a 10-node MicroCloud environment and approximately 1,700 production VMs rebuilt/migrated in under four weeks. Canonical announced new MicroCloud and LXD LTS releases in October 2026. [S16, S19]

Assessment: Credible for sovereign, campus/research and infrastructure automation scenarios where open systems and cloud primitives are priorities. Distinguish MicroCloud's lightweight cluster approach from full OpenStack's infrastructure-service complexity.

Key trade-off: The organization assumes architecture and integration responsibilities that may be bundled or productized in VMware/Nutanix, unless a managed Canonical deployment is contracted.

4.8 Scale Computing Platform / HyperCore

Documented: Scale Computing documents a migration product supporting VMware, Hyper-V and others. Its published customer examples and awards demonstrate commercial use for distributed sites, but no comparable audited global installed base was verified. [S17]

Assessment: A credible evaluation option for distributed manufacturing and remote sites with limited on-site IT staff. Generalizing edge deployments to an entire global datacenter estate would overstate the evidence.

Key trade-off: Integrated simplicity may constrain specialized hardware, network overlays, scale-out architecture and some enterprise tooling choices.

4.9 Vates VMS / XCP-ng

Documented: Vates reports Virtual Computing migrated 550 VMware VMs to its platform, with attributable testimony from the CTO. FUJIFILM MicroChannel also describes replacing VMware with Vates VMS. Both sources were opened; they are vendor-hosted customer statements, not third-party audits. [S14, S18]

Assessment: A credible commercially supported open-source alternative for defined virtualization estates and service providers, including cases with hundreds of VMs. Enterprise teams should test support responsiveness, large-disks, backup compatibility, guest drivers and multi-site DR.

Key trade-off: More flexibility may require assembling an ecosystem that VMware customers obtain in integrated product/support packages.

4.10 VergeOS

Documented: VergeIO's January 2026 press release quotes Alinsco Insurance's CIO and says Alinsco completed migration from VMware on VxRail to VergeOS. Named customer stories include other migrations, but disclosures remain vendor-selected. [S15]

Assessment: Relevant where replacing separate hypervisor, SAN/storage and networking products in one project could simplify the operating model. Particularly worth exploring for well-bounded private-cloud or service-provider requirements.

Key trade-off: Smaller supplier scale and tighter all-in-one integration increase the importance of financial durability, interop, exit portability, supported guest application lists and independent proof of recoverability.

Original Report 5 — OT application-vendor evidence and compatibility catalogs

6A. Published OT and industrial-software virtualization support: what is actually available

Answer: Yes, industrial software publishers provide product- and version-specific virtualization support lists and compatibility documents. No verified cross-vendor universal “OT applications certified on all ten hypervisors” list was found in the original sources reviewed. A competitor claiming to host Windows/Linux guests does not imply a named industrial application vendor will support it.

Does not name AHV, Proxmox, OpenShift Virtualization, HPE HVM, Oracle KVM, Scale, XCP-ng, VergeOS or Canonical. The guide itself is a distinct source and needs its version-specific provisions checked before claiming certification of System Platform, Historian, InTouch or Plant SCADA on a given release. A direct reopen failed, so the help article is an access-limited lead, not fully validated in this audit. Siemens Desigo CC help, OT06 (indexed; direct open failed)

Interpretation for manufacturing and regulated operations: In this inspected set, Microsoft Hyper-V has explicit documented support or vendor guidance in multiple Siemens/Schneider/AVEVA product lines and Rockwell's FactoryTalk guidance, plus specific Emerson DeltaV simulation functions. Nutanix is expressly mentioned in Rockwell's general guide, but other products require exact vendor confirmation. Lack of a product on a published list is not proof it cannot run; it is evidence that this documentation does not establish vendor support.

6A.2 Published compatibility catalogs from the ten competitor platforms

Competitor Catalog or compatibility documentation located Does this prove Siemens/Rockwell/AVEVA/Emerson OT support? Correct diligence action
Nutanix AHV Nutanix product and ecosystem/reference material; Rockwell OT03 names Nutanix in its general support guidance Only product/version-specific confirmation can establish support. Do not promote the generic Rockwell wording to unconditional AHV approval. Ask Rockwell and each OT ISV for an explicit hypervisor/version/guest matrix for each product.
Microsoft Hyper-V/Azure Local Siemens OT01/OT02 and Schneider OT05 explicitly name Hyper-V; Rockwell OT03 also names it Only for those releases and tested configurations. Azure Local is Hyper-V-based but is not automatically listed in each ISV's approved configurations. Confirm Azure Local release, firmware/hardware, guest OS, Hyper-V generation, licensing and OT ISV support.
Red Hat OpenShift Virtualization Red Hat certified ecosystem catalog (opened); Red Hat VM compatibility guidance (opened) No. Catalog includes general ISVs, infrastructure and support integrations; not a blanket industrial application support list. Check exact ISV, guest OS, drivers, network latency and storage performance; request written support exceptions if needed.
HPE Morpheus VM Essentials HPE HVM product/HCL/feature documentation (existing report S10) No verified broad OT application certification list. HPE VM Essentials' management of vCenter is not application certification on HVM. Validate target HVM hypervisor, device support and OT ISV approval independently of HPE control plane.
Proxmox VE Proxmox KVM/cluster documentation and customer directory No universal named OT ISV certification list validated Ask ISVs for exact written support, test USB/dongle/pass-through/clock behavior and recovery, preserve version baselines.
Oracle Linux KVM/OLVM Oracle migration guide S30; Oracle Linux compatibility material No universal OT ISV certification list validated Distinguish Oracle VirtualBox support in Schneider OT04 from OLVM/KVM; obtain product-specific ISV statements.
Canonical MicroCloud/OpenStack Canonical infrastructure guidance and OS/cloud compatibility material No universal OT ISV certification list validated Validate industrial control app, guest OS and relevant I/O/latency and site continuity design.
Scale Computing HyperCore Scale's platform and migration technical material No universal OT ISV certification list validated Seek exact MES/SCADA/historian supported versions and customer references for plant workloads.
Vates VMS/XCP-ng Vendor platform and Linux/Windows guest documentation No universal OT ISV certification list validated Seek written ISV support and validate availability, storage and lifecycle controls.
VergeOS Product/vendor case studies and virtualization documentation No universal OT ISV certification list validated Test app compatibility and supplier support separately from infrastructure migration success.

Important qualification: The table records what was found and checked in the scoped research, not a definitive claim that no compatibility certifications exist anywhere, including vendor login portals, licensed knowledgebases or unpublished customer support letters.

6A.3 OT acceptance gate for a candidate VMware replacement

Before choosing a competitor for a manufacturing or regulated environment, capture all of the following for each application and version: vendor name/product/build, validated guest OS and drivers, named supported hypervisor/version, host/hardware compatibility, VM image/device dependencies (USB dongles, serial, hardware passthrough), controller/field networking topology, clock accuracy, supported snapshots/backup and restore, availability architecture, downtime rules, customer support entitlement, and written change-impact/GxP requalification responsibility. Test restore and rollback; confirm the OEM will support an incident after cutover. Different sites may need different certifications.

Outstanding evidence gaps (research findings, not user-assigned tasks)

  • 18/19 organizations lack a publicly evidenced post-acquisition VMware strategy decision in the accessible records assembled here.
  • No comparable VM/core-count time series for these companies exists in the verified evidence assembled here.
  • No original invoices, VCF contracts, customer-specific renewal prices, or company-wide license inventories were discovered.
  • Gilead residual VMware capacity, per-workload compliance handling, migration savings and procurement effect are not publicly established from the accessible AWS summary.
  • Teva and Lilly vacancy leads are not link-accessible originals in this run; historical indexed snippets are retained only as leads.
  • GSK Azure Local source is Microsoft-authored; no original GSK site-specific VM migration detail was verified.
  • Private cloud, manufacturing OT, research HPC and ordinary public-cloud applications are not comparable measures of virtualization footprint.

Research assurance statement

  • Companies represented: 19 of 19.
  • Opened and inspected original source pages in register: 27 unique entries, including 3 financial/investor records and the core AWS/Microsoft/Roche/Novo original publications. Opened means source content was retrieved through the research tool; not necessarily browser-visible from every network.
  • Claim-level entries with inspected supporting sources: 24.
  • Direct company/SEC original evidence: Broadcom (SEC, retrospectively confirming acquisition), Amgen (SEC), J&J (investor release and historical cloud announcement), AbbVie (expired recruitment), Roche, Novo Nordisk, Takeda (historical strategy).
  • Vendor-hosted or customer-attributed material: AWS/Microsoft customer cases and event summaries, individually flagged V1 or C2 where directly attributable.
  • Inaccessible/search-only/rejected leads: 10 recorded in Appendix D, including the user-flagged Gilead PDF, Lilly job listing, and Broadcom investor release.
  • Claims corrected relative to earlier drafts: Gilead exact 2,200/340 replaced with accessible AWS >2,000/>1,400 figures; Lilly/Teva current footprint downgraded; Amgen funding scenario removed as executed migration; Takeda 80% reclassified as an original *target*; Vertex chronology marked pre-acquisition.
  • Financial coverage limitation: This was not an exhaustive opening of every annual report, quarterly filing, and earnings transcript for all 19 companies; the records actually inspected are enumerated above. No company-specific VMware spend or customer-list extrapolation is made.
  • Overall conclusion: Completed source-qualified public-evidence benchmark, not a fully auditable census of private VMware infrastructure.
  • Research cutoff: 28 October 2026.

*Prepared for METI enterprise cloud technology sensing. Evidence-bounded analyst interpretation is explicitly differentiated from sourced facts. No platform-selection recommendation or unverifiable industry prevalence estimate is made.*

R2 — Broadcom/VMware: primary source register and claim crosswalk

6. Primary research and evidence methodology

1. Population separation: historical VMware installed base vs the selected top-10,000 cohort vs named reference examples. 2. Metric separation: customer count, subscriptions purchased, revenue recognized, licensed cores, contract duration and workloads in production. 3. Scope discipline: Broadcom's software segment includes other businesses; a company's VCF announcement may cover only one country, plant or workload domain. 4. Evidence hierarchy: SEC original filing (F1); Broadcom original company assertion (V1); customer-attributable quotation within company release (C2); secondary-published CEO transcript (S1 for publication, C2 for attributed statement). Neither company nor vendor statements independently audit production claims. 5. Accessibility policy: *Opened original* means the original page's relevant content was retrieved and read. *Official indexed content; full-page open failed* means the publisher's matching text was discoverable in search but complete page retrieval failed. *Opened secondary transcript* is neither an original Broadcom-hosted call record nor an independent audit. 6. Temporal boundary: all claims through 28 October 2026; do not import later information or project FY2026 financial results beyond the latest filed quarter.

Appendix C. Research assurance, source caveats and decision boundaries

Coverage: 19/19 companies represented; all have differentiated evidence or an explicit data limitation. Competitor/reference searches covered Broadcom/VMware, Nutanix, Microsoft, AWS, Google Cloud, Gartner, ETR, targeted Red Hat/other virtualization queries and regulatory/investor sources. This is not a claim that every conference session, enterprise filing, procurement database or paid technographic feed was exhaustively searched.

Opened-source log: E01–E32 comprise 32 designated entries, with E02 partially inspected; E32 is the late-addition AbbVie source. Supporting HTML in E01, E03–E32 was opened in this research run. Some pages are original publication with older event dates; vendor pages can change after publication. The inaccessible originals and uncaptured private records are listed R/X.

No private installed-base census: Broadcom does not identify all VMware customers in its public disclosures; customer-specific contracts, renewal dates, deployed VM/host counts and per-business-unit topologies for these 19 are generally not public. Even a paid technographics estimate requires original proof for a material executive assertion.

Source independence: VMware, AWS, Microsoft, Nutanix and Google Cloud customer stories are commercially interested publications. Customer quotations within them are useful attributed testimony but not an independent audit. ETR is independent *survey research*, subject to sampling/questionnaire limits. SEC filings establish what issuers disclosed, not an externally audited customer-by-customer hypervisor migration register.

Final statement suitable for an executive: Within the 19 biopharma group, the strongest directly published VMware-origin migration example is Gilead. Roche Diagnostics has a specific, quantifiable historical VMware virtualization case, while GSK demonstrates a named alternative on-premises/edge platform and Novo Nordisk/Moderna illustrate purposeful multi-provider deployments for defined workloads. Public disclosures do not support a defensible company-by-company classification of 2026 VMware renewals and exits for all 19. Evidence-backed platform archetypes and transition cases are the appropriate output—not an invented “19-company stay/move scoreboard.”

Research cutoff: 28 October 2026. No recommendations about the sponsoring enterprise's infrastructure decision are made.

Appendix C — Research limitations and assurance statement

  • Ten alternatives are a chosen analytical shortlist, not a validated global market-share ranking. No percentages were invented.
  • Company customer cases are supplier-selected, often with named customer quotations. They support that claims were published, not independent field-test validation, universal application eligibility or an industry migration rate.
  • OT compatibility is software/version/topology-specific. Industrial product publishers' wording, and the exact support agreement, supersede generic hypervisor guest OS support.
  • Nutanix customers and Proxmox servers are unlike denominators. Neither independently measures AHV/Proxmox paid VM market share or VMware churn.
  • Operational equivalence is unproven without live estate testing. HA/DR, storage/network/security features, automation and governance must be tested as a stack.
  • Some official original pages were indexed but not reopened in this research pass; the register records this explicitly. They do not substitute for opened source evidence.
  • No claim of a universal 2026 OT-approved vendor list is made. The research identified product/version-specific publisher statements and an operational validation method.

Research assurance conclusion: Ten competing platform families assessed; material customer-migration claims backed by opened named original customer/supplier publications where indicated; key OT publisher documents reopened, including visual inspection of Rockwell PDF page 9; inaccessible sources downgraded; comparative customer-market share not asserted where data are incompatible. Suitable for a qualified executive decision discussion and to authorize focused technical/commercial validation; not sufficient by itself to certify a target architecture or execute a manufacturing migration.

Assurance limits

  • Named customer cases generally come from suppliers, with attributable customer statements where noted; none was independently audited here.
  • Public product documentation proves availability of a capability in a documented configuration, not universal compatibility or parity with a deployed VMware estate.
  • Market-share figures were not invented to fill the gap in publicly comparable data.
  • Source-access limitations are retained; some valid official links may be restricted by automated retrieval or later site updates.
  • No claims are made that these ten are the ten largest vendors worldwide by customer count or revenue.
  • This is a peer-intelligence shortlist and technical analysis, not procurement due diligence, regulatory validation or a benchmark of independent field testing.

Research assurance conclusion (revised): Ten alternatives assessed; OT compatibility reviewed against original Siemens, Rockwell, Schneider, AVEVA and Emerson documentation; named VMware-displacement cases traced to specific competitor-published reports; inaccessible sources explicitly downgraded; original publisher records used for all ten; sources explicitly labelled by retrieval and provenance; market share intentionally not asserted where the evidence is non-comparable. Suitable as an evidence-qualified executive discussion paper, not a definitive 2026 vendor market-share census or platform certification.

Research assurance and remaining limitations

  • Original publisher full pages opened for key substantive secondary study and HPE methodology: S03 and S09; S&P official 451 archive opened separately.
  • Publisher-authored survey figures identified, but not fully reopened at original article URL: S01/S02. The principal 43% chart remains directly attributed to the publisher and accessible in its indexed publication text, with the access limitation disclosed rather than concealed.
  • Full underlying 451 survey instruments, crosstabs, weightings and question-level bases: not available through the public summary; cannot independently re-estimate subpopulation confidence intervals.
  • Like-for-like 2026 worldwide product revenue/production-VM share tables: not validated in the accessible record.
  • Biopharma-specific breakdown: not established by the reviewed general-enterprise surveys.
  • No market-share time series invented: different studies measure different populations, products and denominators; a 2023-to-2026 line would be methodologically unsupported.

Overall finding: Credible 2026 original research supports VMware's lead in surveyed primary-platform choice and substantial private-cloud installed presence, with meaningful competitive pressure and mixed virtualization strategies. It does not establish a precise worldwide revenue/VM market-share series or customer-loss rate. This is the evidence-grounded conclusion suitable for Cloud Leadership.

F.2 Original customer-response classification (V2.0)

Response pattern sought by leadership Evidence standard Finding from existing research
Expanding or making a new VMware commitment Named VCF or VMware agreement, product selection or reported expanded deployment Documented, but different customers are at agreement, selection, rollout or operating stages. Examples below.
Retaining and optimizing VMware Explicitly documented continued VMware estate plus optimization of its footprint or operating model Not conclusively classified for a named customer from the current case set; a renewal alone does not prove optimization.
Keeping VMware while reducing dependence Explicit partial displacement and evidence that VMware is retained for the remaining scope Not conclusively established as a completed customer-response category in the selected named cases; migration activity without quantified residual footprint does not qualify.
Migrating selected workloads off VMware Named original VMware source, target platform and a reported migration action Documented for Gilead, MSIG Asia, Estracom, HorizonIQ, Virtual Computing and other defined cases.
Completed replacement of a defined estate Named VMware-to-target cutover and explicit completed boundary Supplier-reported for Alinsco's VMware/VxRail estate. Does not establish all enterprise workloads or an independent zero-downtime audit.
Not publicly established Insufficient customer-specific evidence for the proposed response category Unclassified, not assumed to be retaining or exiting. This includes many of the 19 biopharma peer responses.

F.3 Detailed proposed OT compatibility research and standardized evidence record (V2.0)

6.2 How to expand compatibility research across other OT applications

This is a proposed evidence-gathering method, not a claim that the additional product documentation has already been verified.

Application category Research targets Evidence required before classifying compatibility
MES and manufacturing operations Named MES product, release and validated site configuration Manufacturer support matrix, supported guest/hypervisor and upgrade/change conditions
SCADA/HMI and visualization Product-specific runtime, engineering and operator components Manufacturer-tested hypervisor, graphics/remote access, networking and support terms
DCS/process control Controllers, simulation/test and production functions separately Explicit production-versus-simulation scope, deterministic I/O, redundancy and recovery statements
Historians and industrial data Specific historian product/build and middleware/DB stack OS, guest, virtual I/O, storage/performance and resilience support
Laboratory systems and instrument services LIMS, instrument integration, specialist simulation software Guest/device/driver support, hardware peripherals, qualification and retention needs
Industrial supporting infrastructure Network management, identity, site/edge services and virtual appliances Named virtual appliance image, release support, host and recovery constraints

Classify each result using the following evidence states: (a) manufacturer explicitly supports the exact configuration; (b) manufacturer reports a tested configuration with qualifications; (c) general virtualization guidance exists without exact application/release approval; (d) support not established from the inspected record. “Not established” is not the same as “unsupported” or “technically impossible.”

Proposed standardized compatibility record

Field Required content
Application and vendor Exact product/module, manufacturer and use case
Release / operating state App build, guest OS, middleware, site and regulated scope
Hypervisor support Exact named hypervisor, release, virtual hardware and host configurations
Support status and evidence Manufacturer support/tested/general/not established; document URL, version and date
Operational dependencies Clock, devices/dongles, passthrough, I/O, snapshots, HA/DR and backup constraints
Qualification and approval GxP/change-impact requirements, written OEM/ISV confirmation and application-owner sign-off

Conclusion — analyst synthesis

The current research identifies several manufacturer-supported virtualization possibilities but does not yet supply a comprehensive OT application compatibility matrix across all ten alternatives. The practical next step is a product/version-specific source register for the actual application landscape, prioritizing OEM support and qualification evidence before any production hypervisor migration decision.

8. Fixed exceptions register (no repeated search required)

E-01 LOW — wording exception identified, report currently unchanged V2.3 Executive finding 7 reads approximately 100 VMs moved from VMware to Proxmox; alternative table and Annex G similarly suggest migration volume equals the approximately 100-VM environment. The original Proacta case clearly reports VMware-to-Proxmox migration and a resulting estate with approximately 100 VMs across six nodes/two data-center locations, but does not separately enumerate exactly how many VMs were migrated. Single editorial wording correction recommended: “Proacta reports migration from VMware to Proxmox; the resulting environment runs approximately 100 VMs across two data centers; the exact migrated count is not specified.” Apply identically to Executive Summary, competitor row and N23:10 claim ledger. Do not repeat research.
E-02 SOURCE-ACCESS LIMITATION — recorded, not an allegation of false claims AWS/Gilead and Honeywell original PDF text retrievable; web screenshot utility returns redirect/cache errors. Intel 2011 original PDF text retrievable; screenshot also failed. Publisher's own PDFs provide source text. Gilead slide PDF index 17, 20; Honeywell October 2025 printed pp16 and 21 (PDF indexes 15 and 20). The document already explicitly calls out screenshot limitation. No follow-up research cycle. Retain “publisher original PDF text inspected; visual verification unavailable” until someone independently visual-inspects a stable original PDF. Do not characterize as fully visually audited.
E-03 QUALIFICATION, NOT DEFECT — frozen Several 2026-accessible company career pages could not be directly reopened from their original URLs in the latest browser attempt. Original employer-authored text discoverable via publisher indexing; these only support job responsibilities/skill requirements, not confirmed installed VMware estates. Preserve original-source link, publication date if established, and indexed-only status; do not count as contracts or VM installations.
E-04 ANALYTICAL LIMITATION, NOT DEFECT — frozen No published comparable active VMware customer-count, full-company biopharma renewal record, global production VM share or homogeneous 10-supplier viability series. Inconclusive is the correct answer where the evidence does not supply the denominator. Do not invent churn, peer adoption counts, market-share rankings or vendor economic scores. No change without a newly sourced same-denominator original dataset.

Material blockers: No additional material contradiction identified from the reviewed claims and available original records. Outstanding wording: E-01, narrowly scoped. Source limitations E-02–E-04 are disclosed limitations, not reasons to indefinitely postpone the briefing.

9. Final disposition for Cloud Leadership

Leadership research briefing — suitable with qualifications. V2.3 supplies named customer actions, a detailed Gilead case, historical and operational biopharma indicators, alternative-platform migration evidence and exact OT manufacturer records. It does not quantify industry retention/exit behavior or select a preferred platform for an undisclosed enterprise estate.

Do not require another broad verification pass to make that editorial fix. The source/research workstream is now closed at the above evidence cutoff.
